Stinespring’s construction as an adjunction
Arthur J. Parzygnat

Abstract
Given a representation of a unital C ∗ C^{*} -algebra 𝒜 {{\mathcal{A}}} on a Hilbert space ℋ {{\mathcal{H}}} , together with a bounded linear map V : 𝒦 ​ ​ ℋ V:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}} from some other Hilbert space, one obtains a completely positive map on 𝒜 {{\mathcal{A}}} via restriction using the adjoint action associated to V V . We show this restriction forms a natural transformation from a functor of C ∗ C^{*} -algebra representations to a functor of completely positive maps. We exhibit Stinespring’s construction as a left adjoint of this restriction. Our Stinespring adjunction provides a universal property associated to minimal Stinespring dilations and morphisms of Stinespring dilations. We use these results to prove the purification postulate for all finite-dimensional C ∗ C^{*} -algebras.



1
Introduction and outline

Given a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and a state (a positive
and unital linear functional) on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
, the Gelfand–Naimark–Segal (GNS) construction produces a
cyclic representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
in a natural way
compatible with the operation that produces a state from a
representation together with a unit vector.
This naturality has been expressed as an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CAT}}
\textstyle{\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}}

(1.1)

in a certain 2-category of functors from
the (opposite of the) category of
C^{*}
-algebras to the
2-category of locally small categories
1
1

1

Technically, the collection of objects of the 2-category of locally small categories does not form a class. See Sections 3.49, 3.50, and 3.51 in
Adamek et al. 2006
and Sections 8 and 16 in
Shulman 2008
for the set-theoretic foundations of categories of categories and functor categories. In the present work, our constructions will be explicitly defined on objects and morphisms, and these set-theoretic issues will not affect our results.

Parzygnat 2018
.

Stinespring’s construction can be viewed as a generalization
of the GNS construction
by replacing states with operator-valued completely positive (OCP) maps.
In the present work, we extend our GNS adjunction
in Theorem
5.5
to include such OCP maps,
showing that Stinespring’s construction can also be viewed
as an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CAT}}
\textstyle{\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{OCP}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}}

(1.2)

in the same 2-category of functors.

There are several subtle differences between the two adjunctions
(
1.1
) and (
1.2
).
The most notable one is that the category of OCP maps
for a given
C^{*}
-algebra
is no longer discrete as it is for the GNS construction.
This is a consequence of relaxing the unitality assumption on the positive maps and allowing the maps to be operator-valued instead of complex-valued.
Furthermore, for similar reasons, the category of anchored representations has far more morphisms than the category of pointed representations introduced in
Parzygnat 2018
.
Our result provides a
categorical sense of the minimality of Stinespring’s construction
including many of its functorial properties for all
C^{*}
-algebras. As a consequence, we apply our results to prove a version of the purification postulate, which has been used by Chiribella, D’Ariano, and Perinotti as a crucial postulate in isolating quantum theory among other operational probabilistic theories
Chiribella et al. 2010
.

Our results are closely related to, but distinct from, the universal property of
minimal Stinespring dilations
described by Westerbaan and Westerbaan
in
Westerbaan and Westerbaan 2017
(see also Section 2.3 in
Westerbaan 2019
).
In
Westerbaan and Westerbaan 2017
, the authors describe a
universal property for (minimal)
Paschke dilations
for
normal

completely positive maps between
von Neumann algebras
.
In the present work,
we provide an alternative universal property for Stinespring dilations without restricting ourselves to
normal
completely positive maps, since we allow the domains of our maps to be arbitrary
C^{*}
-algebras.
Therefore, we characterize
all
minimal Stinespring dilations by our universal property.
However, our codomain for a completely positive map is assumed to be bounded operators on some Hilbert space, while Westerbaan and Westerbaan assume an abstract von Neumann algebra. Therefore, our results both provide a similar universal property for minimal Stinespring dilations of normal completely positive maps, but neither result subsumes the other. It would be interesting to see if there is a common generalization of both our results that includes Paschke dilations of arbitrary completely positive maps between arbitrary
C^{*}
-algebras.

The outline of our paper is as follows. In Section
2
, relevant background for
completely positive maps is provided. An appropriate category of OCP maps for a
C^{*}
-algebra is defined and briefly contrasted with the
category of states introduced in
Parzygnat 2018
.
In Section
3
, the category of anchored representations
for a
C^{*}
-algebra is introduced. Anchored representations are
generalizations of pointed representations described in
Parzygnat 2018
.
Restriction from anchored representations to OCP maps
is defined in Section
4
and is shown to form a natural
transformation. Section
5
contains our main result, Theorem
5.5
, which states that
the restriction natural transformation has a left adjoint whose ingredients are determined by Stinespring’s construction.
Section
6
provides some immediate consequences of our adjunction, including its universal property.
We provide a comparison to
minimal Stinespring dilations in
Corollary
6.8
.
The rest of Section
6
introduces partial isometries and their role in our Stinespring adjunction. For example, Theorem
6.13
shows that all morphisms between Stinespring dilations extend a minimal intertwining partial isometry.
The first part of Section
7

compares our main theorem to the GNS adjunction from
Parzygnat 2018
.
We then give a reformulation of the standard purification postulate suitable for our purposes in Postulate
7.13
.
What follows is a detailed analysis of how this postulate is a consequence of our results. This leads to Theorem
7.22
and Corollary
7.13
, which establish the essential uniqueness of purifications for finite-dimensional
C^{*}
-algebras.
An index of notation is included in Appendix
A
for the reader’s convenience.
Appendix
B
briefly reviews oplax-natural transformations, modifications, and 2-categorical adjunctions. Proposition
B.10
provides an equivalence in data regarding adjunctions in 2-categories of functors and their evaluations on objects.

In this article, all
C^{*}
-algebras and
∗
-homomorphisms
will be taken to be unital unless otherwise specified.
A
∗
-homomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras
will be denoted diagrammatically as a straight arrow
,
while a linear map (often a completely positive map) of
C^{*}
-algebras
will be denoted as a curvy arrow
\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!.

This is largely motivated by the equivalence between the
(opposite of the) category
of compact Hausdorff spaces with stochastic maps
and the category of commutative
C^{*}
-algebras with positive maps
Parzygnat 2017
,
Furber and Jacobs 2015

This equivalence restricts to the usual commutative Gelfand–Naimark theorem

of compact Hausdorff spaces with continuous functions
and the category of commutative
C^{*}
-algebras with
∗
-homomorphisms
Gelfand and Neumark 1943
. To the best of the author’s knowledge,
the usage of the

notation originated in the work
of Baez and Fritz on relative entropy
Baez and Fritz 2014
.

The symbols

2
Operator-valued completely positive maps on
C^{*}
-algebras

The notion of a completely positive (CP) map will be used throughout
and will therefore be briefly reviewed.
Most of the concepts used here are introduced in the first few chapters
of Paulsen
Paulsen 2003
.
General facts used without mention regarding Hilbert spaces can be found in Chapter 4 of Rudin
Rudin 1987
and Chapter 12 of Rudin
Rudin 1991
.

Notation 2.1
.

The set of natural numbers
1,2,3,\dots
is denoted by
{{\mathbb{N}}}
.
If
{{\mathcal{K}}}
is a Hilbert space,
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}})
denotes the
C^{*}
-algebra of bounded operators
on
{{\mathcal{K}}}.
If
a\in{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}}),
then
a^{*}
denotes the adjoint of
a.

If
{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a
C^{*}
-algebra,
1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
denotes the unit in
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

The norm on
C^{*}
-algebras
is always written using
\lVert\;\cdot\;\rVert
without any subscripts,
while norms and inner products on Hilbert spaces frequently have subscripts. For example,
\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
denotes the inner product
on
{{\mathcal{K}}}
with linearity in the right variable and conjugate
linearity in the left variable.
For
n\in{{\mathbb{N}}},

{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})
denotes the
C^{*}
-algebra of
n\times n
matrices
with entries in
{{\mathcal{A}}}
. Addition and multiplication in
\mathcal{M}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})
are defined as for ordinary matrices. If
a_{ij}
is the
ij
-th entry of
A\in\mathcal{M}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}}),
then the
ij
-th entry of
A^{*}
is
a_{ji}^{*}
.
The norm on
{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is used only once in this article
(cf. (
5.16
))
and the reader is referred to the first several pages in
Chapter 1 of Paulsen
Paulsen 2003
for details.
In particular,
{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathbb{C}}})
denotes the
C^{*}
-algebra of

n\times n
complex matrices. In this case, the unit of

{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathbb{C}}})
will be denoted by
\mathds{1}_{n}.
Any
C^{*}
-algebra of the form
\mathcal{M}_{n}({{\mathbb{C}}})
will be referred to as a matrix algebra.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and
{{\mathcal{B}}}
be
C^{*}
-algebras,
and let
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}
be a linear map.
The map
\varphi
is said to be
unital
iff
\varphi(1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}})=1_{{{\mathcal{B}}}}.

An element of a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
is
positive

iff it equals
a^{*}a
for some
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
. A linear map
between
C^{*}
-algebras is
positive
iff it is linear and
it sends positive elements to positive elements.
A positive map into
{{\mathbb{C}}}
is referred to as a
positive linear functional
. A positive unital linear functional is called a
state
.
If
n\in{{\mathbb{N}}}
, the
n
-ampliation
of
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}
is the linear map

\varphi_{n}:{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{B}}})

the assignment

(2.3)

The map
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}

The map
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}

is said to be
completely positive

iff it is
n
-positive for all
n\in{{\mathbb{N}}}.

The shorthand PU, CP, and CPU may be used to denote a
positive unital, completely positive, or completely positive unital map,
respectively.

When
{{\mathcal{B}}}={{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}}),
bounded operators on a Hilbert space
{{\mathcal{K}}},

the latter matrix in (
2.3
)
acts on an
n

{{\mathcal{K}}},
i.e. elements of
{{\mathcal{K}}}\oplus\cdots\oplus{{\mathcal{K}}}
(
n

another
n
-tuple of vectors in
{{\mathcal{K}}}

(one chooses an ordering for the direct sum here).

Example 2.1
.

Some examples of CP maps follow.

(a)

All

C^{*}
-algebras are CPU.

(b)

Let

is CP.

.

Lemma 2.2
.

Each of the three classes of maps between
C^{*}
-algebras from Definition
2.2
(positive, unital, and CP) is closed under composition.
All positive maps
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}
between
C^{*}
-algebras
are self-adjoint in the sense that
\varphi(a^{*})=\varphi(a)^{*}
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Proof 2.3
.

See Exercise 2.1 in Paulsen
Paulsen 2003
for the last statement.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
C^{*}
-algebra. An
operator-valued CP map
(OCP map) on
{{\mathcal{A}}}

is a pair
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
consisting of a Hilbert space
{{\mathcal{K}}}

and a CP map

\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}}).

When
\varphi
is unital, it is called an
operator state
on
{{\mathcal{A}}}

Effros 1978
.
Let
({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)
be another OCP map on
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

A
morphism of OCP maps

T:({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)

is a bounded linear map

T:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{L}}}
such that

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi(a)}
\scriptstyle{\psi(a)}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{T}

(2.5)

commutes for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.
If
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
and
({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)
are operator states, a
morphism of operator states
is a morphism of OCP maps with
T
an isometry.

Remark 2.5
.

The choice of morphisms of OCP maps is subtle.
One might have tried any number of reasonable variants.
For instance, one might request that the diagram

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}})}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{L}}})}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}

\scriptstyle{\psi}

\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ad}_{T}}

(2.6)

commutes. Another possibility is that the diagram

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}})}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{L}}})}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}

\scriptstyle{\psi}

\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ad}_{T^{*}}}

(2.7)

commutes.
When
T
is an isometry,
commutativity of (
2.6
)
implies commutativity of (
2.5
) and commutativity of
(
2.5
)
implies commutativity of
(
2.7
). When
T^{*}
is an isometry, these implications are reversed. In particular, when
T
is unitary, all three are equivalent.
To see the first implication when
T
is an isometry, diagram (
2.6
) says

T\varphi(a)T^{*}=\psi(a)
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Applying
T
on the right gives

T\varphi(a)=\psi(a)T
because
T^{*}T=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}.

The second implication follows by applying
T^{*}
on the left of this result.
In summary (
2.6
)
\implies
(
2.5
)
\implies
(
2.7
) for operator states.
Explicit counter-examples showing the reverse implications
fail in general are provided in Examples
2.8
and
2.9
.
We will see that
commutativity of (
2.6
)
is too strong a requirement and
commutativity of (
2.7
)
is too weak a requirement for the purposes sought out in this work
(these points will be explained in footnotes).
No such simple comparison can be made for OCP maps.
2
2

2

This is easy to see for trivial reasons. For example, if
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)
is a morphism of OCP maps and
\lambda\in[0,1)\cup(1,\infty)
, then
({{\mathcal{K}}},\lambda\varphi)\xrightarrow{\lambda T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\lambda\psi)
is a morphism of OCP maps. However, neither
(
2.6
) nor (
2.7
) commute in general. Along similar lines, (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) do not imply each other (see also Example
2.7
).

These subtle points and the universal properties that will be discussed in this work would have been missed if we demanded
T
to be unitary.

Example 2.6
.

As a special case, set
{{\mathcal{K}}}={{\mathbb{C}}}.

An OCP map
{\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}})\cong{{\mathbb{C}}}}
is (naturally isomorphic to) a positive linear functional.
If
\psi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathbb{C}}}
is another such OCP map, then a morphism

T:({{\mathbb{C}}},\varphi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>({{\mathbb{C}}},\psi)
of OCP maps consists of a linear map
T:{{\mathbb{C}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}.

Such a linear map must be of the form

T(\lambda)=z\lambda
for all
\lambda\in{{\mathbb{C}}}
for some unique
z\in{{\mathbb{C}}}.

If
z\neq 0
, since
\varphi
and
\psi
are linear, the diagram

\textstyle{{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi(a)}
\scriptstyle{\psi(a)}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{T}

(2.8)

commutes for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
if and only if
\varphi=\psi.

Notice that when
T
is an isometry, it is of the form

T(\lambda)=e^{i\theta}\lambda
for some
\theta\in[0,2\pi).
In this case, (
2.7
)
and (
2.6
)
(and hence (
2.5
) as well)
are equivalent because
T
is also unitary.

Example 2.7
.

Fix
m,p\in{{\mathbb{N}}}
and define the
tracial map

\begin{split}{{\mathcal{M}}}_{m}({{\mathbb{C}}})&\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.69989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.84393pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{M}}}_{p}({{\mathbb{C}}})\\
A&\xmapsto{\;\;\hskip 16.38895pt}\frac{1}{m}{\rm tr}(A)\mathds{1}_{p},\end{split}

(2.9)

where
{\rm tr}
denotes the standard (un-normalized) trace.
A quick computation shows
\tau
is unital. To see that it is CP, first note that since the trace is positive,
the trace is CP because every positive map
into
{{\mathbb{C}}}
is automatically CP
(cf. Theorem 3 in Stinespring
Stinespring 1955
).
Second, note that
\tau
equals the composite of CPU maps

\mathcal{M}_{m}({{\mathbb{C}}})\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.69989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.8965pt\raise 5.15279pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\frac{1}{m}{\rm tr}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathbb{C}}}\xrightarrow{\;\;!\;\;}{{\mathcal{M}}}_{p}({{\mathbb{C}}}),

(2.10)

where the second
map is the unique unital linear map sending
1
to
\mathds{1}_{p}.
Therefore,
\tau
is CPU by Example
2.1
and Lemma
2.2
. Given
q\in{{\mathbb{N}}},
let
\sigma:\mathcal{M}_{m}({{\mathbb{C}}})\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!\mathcal{M}_{q}({{\mathbb{C}}})
denote the tracial map for these dimensions and let
T:{{\mathbb{C}}}^{p}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}^{q}
be
any
linear transformation. Then
({{\mathbb{C}}}^{p},\tau)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{q},\sigma)
is a morphism of OCP maps.

Example 2.9
.

The present example will show that

{{\mathcal{A}}}:=\mathcal{M}_{2}({{\mathbb{C}}})
and set

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {{\mathcal{A}}}&\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.69989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.62987pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathbb{C}}}&\qquad{{\mathcal{A}}}&\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.69989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.64584pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!\mathcal{M}_{2}({{\mathbb{C}}})&\qquad{{\mathbb{C}}}&\xrightarrow{\;\;\;\;T\;\;\;\;}{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\\

1&0\end{bmatrix}A\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}&\lambda&\xmapsto{\;\hskip 16.38895pt}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda\\
0\end{bmatrix}.\end{aligned}

(2.15)

Then
\varphi
and
\psi
are operator states (cf. Examples

and
2.1
). Note that
T^{*}:{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}
is given by

T^{*}\left(\begin{bmatrix}x\\
y\end{bmatrix}\right)=x

(2.16)

and

\psi\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}a+d&b+c\\

(2.17)

Therefore, although (

) holds,

\left(T\varphi\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\

c&d\end{bmatrix}\right)T\right)(\lambda)=\frac{\lambda}{2}\begin{bmatrix}a+d\\
b+c\end{bmatrix}

(2.18)

show that condition (
2.5
) fails.

The following proposition provides
the general structure
of morphisms of operator states.

Proposition 2.10
.

Let
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}})
and
\psi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}(\mathcal{L})
be operator states on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
with
\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{L}
Hilbert spaces.
A morphism
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)
of operator states exists if and only if
there exist closed subspaces
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{L},

operator states
\psi_{j}:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}(\mathcal{L}_{j})
for
j\in\{1,2\},
and a unitary
map
U:\mathcal{K}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{L}_{1}
such that

\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{2},

U\varphi(a)U^{*}=\psi_{1}(a),
and

\psi(a)=\psi_{1}(a)\oplus\psi_{2}(a)
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Proof 2.11
.

you found me!

(
\Rightarrow
)
For the forward direction, set
\mathcal{L}_{1}:=T(\mathcal{K}).

Since
T
is an isometry,
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is a closed subspace of
\mathcal{L}
.
Set
\mathcal{L}_{2}:=\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\perp},
the orthogonal complement of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
inside
\mathcal{L},

U:=\pi_{1}T,
and
\psi_{j}(a):=\pi_{j}\psi(a)i_{j}
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}},
where
\pi_{j}:\mathcal{L}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{L}_{j}
denotes the projection onto the
j
-th factor
and
i_{j}:\mathcal{L}_{j}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{L}
denotes the inclusion of the
j
-th factor.
Since
T
is an isometry,
U
is unitary.
Since
T
is a morphism of operator states,
\psi(a)\mathcal{L}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{1}
for all

a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.
Furthermore, since
\psi
is positive,
\psi(a)^{*}=\psi(a^{*})
so that
T^{*}\psi(a^{*})=\varphi(a^{*})T^{*}
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
upon taking the adjoint of (
2.5
). Since
∗
is an involution on
{{\mathcal{A}}},
this is equivalent to
T^{*}\psi(a)=\varphi(a)T^{*}
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.
Thus,
\psi(a)\mathcal{L}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{2}.
These two facts imply
\psi(a)=\psi_{1}(a)\oplus\psi_{2}(a)
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.
Finally,

\begin{split}U\varphi(a)U^{*}&=\pi_{1}T\varphi(a)T^{*}i_{1}\quad\text{ by definition of $U$}\\
&=\pi_{1}TT^{*}\psi(a)TT^{*}i_{1}\quad\text{ by (\ref{eq:opstatesdiagram}) and Remark~\ref{rmk:morphismopstates}}\\
&=\pi_{1}\psi(a)i_{1}\quad\text{ since $T$ is an isometry onto $\mathcal{L}_{1}$}\\
&=\psi_{1}(a)\quad\text{ by definition of $\psi_{j}$}\end{split}

(2.19)

for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

(
\Leftarrow
)
To see the reverse direction, set
T:=i_{1}U
.
Then
T
is a morphism of operator states since the diagram

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi(a)}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{1}(a)}
\scriptstyle{U}
\scriptstyle{U}
\scriptstyle{i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\psi(a)}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{T}

(2.20)

commutes for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

OCP maps and operator states together with their morphisms form categories.

Lemma 2.12
.

Let

({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)\xrightarrow{S}({{\mathcal{M}}},\chi)
be a pair of
composable morphisms of OCP maps (or operator states) on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
. The composite
of said morphisms is defined to be
the composite
ST
of linear transformations and is
a morphism of OCP maps (resp. operator states). The identity

({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}_{({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)

is the identity linear transformation
\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}.

The collection of all OCP maps (or operator states) on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
with their morphisms forms a category, which is
denoted by
\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})
(resp.
\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}})
).
Furthermore,
\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is a subcategory of
\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}}).

Remark 2.13
.

The result
T^{*}\psi(a)=\varphi(a)T^{*}
obtained in the proof of Proposition
2.10
shows
\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is a
∗
-category. This will be explained in more detail in Proposition
6.15
.

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
f:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
∗
-homomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras.
The assignment

\begin{split}\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{OCP}_{f}}\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})\\
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\;}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi\circ f)\\
\Big(({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)\Big)&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\;}\Big(({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi\circ f)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi\circ f)\Big)\end{split}

(2.21)

defines a functor. Furthermore, it restricts to a functor
\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{OpSt}_{f}}\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})
.

Lemma 2.15
.

The assignment

\begin{split}\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{OCP}}\mathcal{CAT}\\
{{\mathcal{A}}}&\xmapsto{\;\;\hskip 8.19447pt\;\;}\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})\\
\left({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}\right)&\xmapsto{\;\;\hskip 8.19447pt\;\;}\left(\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{OCP}_{f}}\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})\right)\end{split}

(2.22)

defines a functor. The same is true for
\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{OpSt}}\mathcal{CAT}
.

3
Anchored representations of
C^{*}
-algebras

Definition 3.1
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
C^{*}
-algebra. An
anchored representation

of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a quadruple
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
consisting of two Hilbert spaces
{{\mathcal{H}}}
and
{{\mathcal{K}}},

a
∗
-homomorphism
\pi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}),
and
a bounded linear map
V:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}.

When
V
is an isometry,
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
is called a
preserving anchored representation
.
Let
({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)
be another anchored representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

A
morphism of anchored representations

(T,L):({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)
consists of bounded linear maps

T:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{L}}}
and
L:{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{I}}}
satisfying the following two conditions.
First, the diagram

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{I}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{I}}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi(a)}
\scriptstyle{\rho(a)}
\scriptstyle{L}
\scriptstyle{L}

(3.1)

commutes for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
. Second, the diagrams

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{I}}}}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{W}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{L}

and

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{I}}}}
\scriptstyle{V^{*}}
\scriptstyle{W^{*}}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{L}

(3.2)

both commute. When
V,W,T,
and
L
are isometries, then
(T,L)
is said to be a
morphism of preserving anchored representations
.

Remark 3.2
.

For preserving anchored representations,
commutativity of the right diagram in
(
3.2
) holds whenever
T
is unitary
and the left diagram in (
3.2
) commutes.
To see this, the left diagram says
WT=LV.

Taking the adjoint of this condition gives
T^{*}W^{*}=V^{*}L^{*}.

Applying
T
on the left and
L
on the right gives

W^{*}L=TV^{*},
which is the diagram on the right in
(
3.2
).

When the bounded linear maps in these definitions are isometries,
the following lemma shows the isometry
T:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{L}}}

is redundant and can be constructed from
L:{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{I}}}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
{{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},{{\mathcal{L}}},
and
{{\mathcal{I}}}
be Hilbert spaces and let
V:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}},

W:{{\mathcal{L}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{I}}},
and
L:{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{I}}}
be isometries.
Then
L
satisfies

L\big(V({{\mathcal{K}}})\big)\subseteq W({{\mathcal{L}}})\quad\text{ and }\quad L\big(V({{\mathcal{K}}})^{\perp}\big)\subseteq W({{\mathcal{L}}})^{\perp}

(3.3)

if and only if
there exists a (necessarily unique) isometry
T:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{L}}}
such that
the diagrams in (
3.2
) commute.

In (
3.3
),
W({{\mathcal{L}}})^{\perp}
stands for the orthogonal complement of
W({{\mathcal{L}}})\subseteq\mathcal{I}
and likewise for
V({{\mathcal{K}}})^{\perp}\subseteq{{\mathcal{H}}}.

Proof 3.4

(Proof of Lemma
3.3
)
.

First note that since
V
and
W
are isometries, their images are closed. Hence,

{{\mathcal{H}}}=V({{\mathcal{K}}})\oplus V({{\mathcal{K}}})^{\perp}\quad\text{ and }\quad{{\mathcal{I}}}=W({{\mathcal{L}}})\oplus W({{\mathcal{L}}})^{\perp}.

(3.4)

(
\Rightarrow
)
Assume
L
satisfies (
3.3
).
Then the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{K}}
\textstyle{V({{\mathcal{K}}})\oplus V({{\mathcal{K}}})^{\perp}}
\textstyle{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}
\textstyle{W({{\mathcal{L}}})\oplus W({{\mathcal{L}}})^{\perp}}
\textstyle{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}V}
\scriptstyle{L}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}Li_{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}}
\scriptstyle{W^{*}}
\scriptstyle{(\pi_{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}W)^{*}\;}
\scriptstyle{i_{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}}

(3.5)

commutes by construction. In this diagram,
\pi_{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}
and
i_{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}
denote projection and inclusion maps, respectively.
Note that
\pi_{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}Li_{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}
is
an isometry because
L\big(V({{\mathcal{K}}})\big)\subseteq W({{\mathcal{L}}})
by (
3.4
).
Setting
T:=W^{*}LV
,
it follows from (
3.5
) that
T
is the composite of the two unitary maps

\pi_{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}V
and
{(\pi_{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}W)^{*}}
and the isometry

\pi_{W({{\mathcal{L}}})}Li_{V({{\mathcal{K}}})}
. Therefore,
T
is an isometry.
Finally, the diagrams in (
3.2
) commute because they are given by

(3.6)

respectively, where

inside
{{\mathcal{I}}}
, and similarly for

(
\Leftarrow
)
Assume an isometry

satisfying (
3.2
)
exists. Commutativity of the diagram on the left requires
L\big(V({{\mathcal{K}}})\big)\subseteq W({{\mathcal{L}}})
, while commutativity of the diagram on the right requires
L\big(V({{\mathcal{K}}})^{\perp}\big)\subseteq W({{\mathcal{L}}})^{\perp}.

Notation 3.7
.

A morphism of preserving anchored representations will be denoted
by the pair
(T,L):({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)
,
even though the isometry
T
is uniquely determined by
L
as illustrated
in Lemma
3.3
. This is because
T
need not be uniquely determined by
L
in the general case of anchored representations and because we will use
T
to relate anchored representations to OCP maps in this work.

Example 3.5
.

In the special case
{{\mathcal{K}}}={{\mathbb{C}}}
,
an anchored representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
consists of
a
∗
-representation
\pi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}})
and
a linear map

V:{{\mathbb{C}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}.

Such a map is uniquely characterized by the vector

\Omega:=V(1)\in{{\mathcal{H}}},
which is a unit vector if and only if
V
is an isometry.
Hence, a preserving anchored representation
of the form
({{\mathbb{C}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)

is equivalent to a pointed representation as introduced in
Definition 5.1 in
Parzygnat 2018
.
If
({{\mathbb{C}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)
is another preserving anchored representation,
then a morphism
({{\mathbb{C}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\xrightarrow{(T,L)}({{\mathbb{C}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)
of preserving anchored representations
consists of isometries
T:{{\mathbb{C}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}
and
L:{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{I}}}

satisfying (
3.1
) and (
3.2
).

T
must be of the form
T(\lambda)=\lambda e^{i\theta}
for all
\lambda\in{{\mathbb{C}}}

for some
\theta\in[0,2\pi).

L
is an intertwiner of representations by (
3.1
).
Let
\Xi:=W(1).
The left diagram in
(
3.2
) entails

L(\Omega)=e^{i\theta}\Xi.

Since
V^{*}=\langle\Omega,\;\cdot\;\rangle
and
W^{*}=\langle\Xi,\;\cdot\;\rangle,

the right diagram in (
3.2
) entails

e^{i\theta}\langle\Omega,\;\cdot\;\rangle=\langle\Xi,L(\;\cdot\;)\rangle
as linear functionals on
{{\mathcal{H}}}.

However, this second condition is implied by the first one in
(
3.2
) by Remark
3.2
. Hence, this reproduces the morphisms of pointed representations in
Parzygnat 2018
up to a phase.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\xrightarrow{(T,L)}({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)\xrightarrow{(S,M)}({{\mathcal{M}}},{{\mathcal{J}}},\sigma,X)

be a pair of
composable morphisms of anchored representations on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
. The composite
of said morphisms is defined as
(S,M)\circ(T,L):=(ST,ML)

and is a morphism of anchored representations.
A similar statement is true for preserving anchored representations and their morphisms.
The identity
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}})}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)

is the identity linear transformation on each Hilbert space.
The collection of all anchored representations on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and their morphisms forms a category, which is
denoted by
\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}).
Similarly, the collection of all preserving anchored representations on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and their morphisms forms a category, which is
denoted by
\mathbf{PAnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}).

Furthermore,
\mathbf{PAnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is a subcategory of
\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})
.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
f:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
∗
-homomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras.
The assignment

\begin{split}\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}}\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})\\
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi\circ f,V)\\
\Big(\!({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\!\xrightarrow{\!(T,L)\!}\!({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)\!\Big)&\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 16.38895pt}\Big(\!({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi\circ f,V)\!\xrightarrow{\!(T,L)\!}\!({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho\circ f,W)\!\Big)\end{split}

(3.8)

defines a functor. Furthermore, it restricts to a functor

\mathbf{PAnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{PAnRep}_{f}}\mathbf{PAnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})
.

Lemma 3.8
.

The assignment

\begin{split}\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{\mathbf{AnRep}}}\mathcal{CAT}\\
{{\mathcal{A}}}&\xmapsto{\;\hskip 16.38895pt\;\;\!}\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})\\
\left({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}\right)&\xmapsto{\;\hskip 16.38895pt\;\;\!}\left(\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}}\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})\right)\end{split}

(3.9)

defines a functor. The same is true for
\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{\mathbf{PAnRep}}}\mathcal{CAT}
.

4
The restriction natural transformation

The follow proposition illustrates how to construct an OCP map from an anchored representation.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
C^{*}
-algebra. The assignment

\begin{split}\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})\\
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\!}({{\mathcal{K}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi)\\
\Big(({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\xrightarrow{(T,L)}({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)\Big)&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\!}\Big(({{\mathcal{K}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{W^{*}}\circ\rho)\Big)\end{split}

(4.1)

defines a functor. Furthermore, it restricts to a functor
\mathbf{PAnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}}).

Here,
(\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi)(a):=V^{*}\pi(a)V
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
(cf. Example
2.1
).

Proof 4.2

(Proof of Proposition
4.1
)
.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
be an anchored representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Then
\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi
is an OCP map because it is the composite of the CP map
\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}

and the
∗
-homomorphism
\pi
.
Since
\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}
is unital if and only if
V
is an isometry,

\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi
is an operator state when
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
is a preserving anchored representation.
Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\xrightarrow{(T,L)}({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{I}}},\rho,W)
be a morphism of anchored representations.
In order for
({{\mathcal{K}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{W^{*}}\circ\rho)

to be a morphism of OCP maps, the diagram

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{L}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}(\pi(a))}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ad}_{W^{*}}(\rho(a))}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{T}

(4.2)

must commute for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.
Expanding out the definition of the adjoint action map
provides the diagram

Lemma 4.3
.

0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72084pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 48.13477pt\raise-6.41524pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
