0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 15.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces}}}},

(4.6)

a special kind of oplax-natural transformation (cf. Definition
B.1
).

5
Stinespring’s oplax-natural transformation

In the construction of a left adjoint to
\mathbf{rest},
some
preliminary facts will be needed.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}})
be a completely positive map.
Let
\vec{v}:=(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})\in{{\mathcal{K}}}\oplus\cdots\oplus{{\mathcal{K}}}

denote a vector in the direct sum of
{{\mathcal{K}}}
with itself
n
times.
Then the assignment

\begin{split}s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}:{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})&\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathbb{C}}}\\
A&\xmapsto{\;\hskip 8.19447pt}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\langle v_{i},\varphi(a_{ij})v_{j}\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\end{split}

(5.1)

is a positive linear functional.

Proof 5.2
.

Suppose
A\in{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is positive.
Then, because
\varphi
is completely positive,

\varphi_{n}(A)\geq 0.
Hence,

s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}(A)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\langle v_{i},\varphi(a_{ij})v_{j}\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}=\langle\vec{v},\varphi_{n}(A)\vec{v}\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}\oplus\cdots\oplus{{\mathcal{K}}}}\geq 0.

(5.2)

Linearity of
s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}
follows from linearity of
\varphi_{n}

and linearity of the inner product in the right variable.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
X
and
Y
be topological vector spaces, let
X\xrightarrow{f}Y
be a continuous linear map, and let
N\subseteq X
be a closed vector subspace of
X.

(a)

If
f(x)=0
for all
x\in N,
then there exists a unique continuous linear map
X/N\xrightarrow{g}Y
such that

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{X/N}
\textstyle{Y}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{g}

(5.3)

commutes. Here,
X/N
is the quotient space of
X
modulo
N
and
X\twoheadrightarrow X/N
is the quotient map.

(b)

If
M\subseteq Y
is a closed subspace of
Y
and
f(N)\subseteq M
, then there exists a unique continuous linear map
X/N\xrightarrow{h}Y/M
such that

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{X/N}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{Y/M}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{h}

(5.4)

commutes.

Proof 5.4
.

For the first fact, see Theorem 1.41 and Exercise 9 in Chapter 1 of Rudin
Rudin 1991
.
The second fact is a consequence of the first.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
M:{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}
a bounded positive operator on a Hilbert space
{{\mathcal{H}}}
. Then
M\leq\lVert M\rVert\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}
(in the sense that
\lVert M\rVert\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}-M
is a positive operator).

Proof 5.6
.

This follows from the inequality
\langle x,Mx\rangle_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}\leq\lVert x\rVert_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}\lVert Mx\rVert_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}\leq\lVert M\rVert\lVert x\rVert_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}^{2}
for all
x\in{{\mathcal{H}}}
by positivity of
M
, Cauchy–Schwarz, and the definition of the norm on
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}).

The following is the main result of this work.

Theorem 5.6
.

There exists a left adjoint
\mathbf{Stine}:\mathbf{OCP}\Rightarrow\mathbf{AnRep}

to the natural transformation
\mathbf{rest}:\mathbf{AnRep}\Rightarrow\mathbf{OCP}

\textstyle{\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CAT}}
\textstyle{\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{OCP}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}}

(5.6)

in the 2-category
\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{CAT}).

Furthermore,
\mathbf{Stine}:\mathbf{OCP}\Rightarrow\mathbf{AnRep}
restricts to a left adjoint
\mathbf{Stine}:\mathbf{OpSt}\Rightarrow\mathbf{PAnRep}
to
\mathbf{rest}:\mathbf{PAnRep}\Rightarrow\mathbf{OpSt}
.

The 2-category
\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{CAT})
is defined in Notation
B.15
and the definition of a 2-categorical adjunction is reviewed in Definition
B.7
. The consequences, universal properties, and applications of this theorem are discussed in Sections
6
and
7
.

Proof 5.7

(Proof of Theorem
5.5
)
.

The proof will be split up into several steps.

i.

For a fixed
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}},
define the functor

\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}:\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})

on objects.

ii.

For a fixed
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}},
define the functor

\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}:\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})

on morphisms and prove functoriality.

iii.

For a fixed
∗
-homomorphism
f:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}},
define the natural transformation

\mathbf{Stine}_{f}:\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\circ\mathbf{OCP}_{f}\Rightarrow\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}\circ\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}.

iv.

Prove that
\mathbf{Stine}
is an oplax-natural transformation
(cf. Definition
B.1
).

v.

For a fixed
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}},

construct the appropriate natural transformation

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}:\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\circ\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Rightarrow\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})}.

vi.

Show that
4
4

4

The vertical concatenation is the vertical composition of
natural transformations. This notation is used in
Parzygnat 2018
and
Parzygnat 2019
. It is also reviewed in Appendix
B
in the present work.
In particular, applying
m
to a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
gives a natural transformation

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}:\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\circ\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Rightarrow\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})}.

m:\begin{matrix}[0.7]\mathbf{rest}\\
\mathbf{Stine}\end{matrix}\Rrightarrow\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{AnRep}}
is a modification
(cf. Definition
B.3
)
between oplax-natural transformations in
the 2-category
\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{CAT}).

vii.

Show that
\begin{matrix}[0.7]\mathbf{Stine}\\
\mathbf{rest}\end{matrix}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{OCP}}.

viii.

Prove the zig-zag identities for adjunctions in 2-categories (cf. Definition
B.7
),
i.e. complete the proof that
(\mathbf{Stine},\mathbf{rest},\mathrm{id},m)

is an adjunction in
\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{CAT}).

In all of the above steps, justifications for reducing to operator states and preserving anchored representations will be provided.
In what follows, if a proof for any claim is not supplied,
it is because the justification is analogous to the
standard GNS construction arguments or it
follows easily from the definitions. The reader is
referred to
Parzygnat 2018
for more details.

i.

The construction of an anchored representation from an OCP map will
be Stinespring’s construction (cf. the proof of sufficiency of Theorem 1 in Stinespring
Stinespring 1955
).
Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
C^{*}
-algebra and let
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
be an OCP map on
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Recall, this means
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}})
is a CP map.
Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}
denote the vector space tensor product of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
with
{{\mathcal{K}}}.

In particular, elements of
{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}
are finite sums of tensor products of vectors
in
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and vectors in
{{\mathcal{K}}}

(in fact, all sums that follow are finite).
The function

\begin{split}({{\mathcal{A}}}\times{{\mathcal{K}}})\times({{\mathcal{A}}}\times{{\mathcal{K}}})&\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}\\
\big((a,v),(b,w)\big)&\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}\langle v,\varphi(a^{*}b)w\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\end{split}

(5.7)

is conjugate bilinear in the first
{{\mathcal{A}}}\times{{\mathcal{K}}}
factor and bilinear in the second
{{\mathcal{A}}}\times{{\mathcal{K}}}
factor. Hence, by the universal property of the algebraic tensor product (cf. Chapter IV Section 5 in Hungerford
Hungerford 1980
), the assignment

\begin{split}({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})\times({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})&\xrightarrow{\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}}{{\mathbb{C}}}\\
\left(\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i},\sum_{j}b_{j}\otimes w_{j}\right)&\xmapsto{\;\;\;\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\;\;\!}\sum_{i,j}\langle v_{i},\varphi(a_{i}^{*}b_{j})w_{j}\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\end{split}

(5.8)

is well-defined, conjugate linear in the first variable,
and linear in the second variable.
Furthermore,
\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}

satisfies
5
5

5

An overline here indicates complex conjugation.
This is not to be confused with the closure such as in (
5.19
).

\langle\!\langle\zeta,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}=\overline{\langle\!\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}}\qquad\forall\;\zeta,\xi\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}.

(5.9)

Since the matrix

A:=\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}^{*}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{1}^{*}a_{n}\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
a_{n}^{*}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}^{*}a_{n}\\
\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}^{*}\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}

(5.10)

in
{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is positive for all
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\in{{\mathcal{A}}},

\langle\!\langle\xi,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}=\sum_{i,j}\langle v_{i},\varphi(a_{i}^{*}a_{j})v_{j}\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\geq 0

(5.11)

by Lemma
5.1
applied to
\xi=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}.

By the properties of
\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi},

it follows that

|\langle\!\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}|^{2}\leq\langle\!\langle\xi,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}\langle\!\langle\zeta,\zeta\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}\qquad\forall\;\xi,\zeta\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}.

(5.12)

This is the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for such sesquilinear forms (cf. Construction 3.1 in
Parzygnat 2018
).
Thus,
\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}
is a sesquilinear form whose associated seminorm endows
{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}
with the structure of a topological vector space.
In general,
\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}
is not positive semi-definite. Hence, set

{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}:=\big\{\zeta\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}\;:\;\langle\!\langle\zeta,\zeta\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}=0\big\}

(5.13)

to be its null-space.
From the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality (
5.12
),
it follows that

\langle\!\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}=0\quad\forall\;\xi\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}},\;\zeta\in{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}.

(5.14)

Using this, one can show that

{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}
is a closed vector subspace of
{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}
(it is closed since it is defined as the inverse image of
\{0\}
under a continuous map).

By the universal property of the tensor product, for each
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}},
the map

\begin{split}{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}&\xrightarrow{\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)}{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}\\
\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}\sum_{i}aa_{i}\otimes v_{i}\end{split}

(5.15)

is a well-defined linear transformation.
If we write
\mathrm{End}(V)
for the algebra of linear transformations
from a vector space
V
to itself, then

\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathrm{End}({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})
defines a
representation of the algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
on the vector space

{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}.

Furthermore, for each
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
and
\xi=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}},

\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)
satisfies

\begin{split}\langle\!\langle\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)\xi,\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}&=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big\langle v_{i},\varphi(a_{i}^{*}a^{*}aa_{j})v_{j}\big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\;\;\;\text{by definition of $\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}$ and $\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}$}\\
&=s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}^{*}\begin{bmatrix}a^{*}a&&0\\
&\ddots&\\
0&&a^{*}a\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}\right)\\
&\leq\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}a^{*}a&&0\\
&\ddots&\\
0&&a^{*}a\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}^{*}\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0\end{bmatrix}\right)\\
&=\lVert a^{*}a\rVert s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}^{*}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{1}^{*}a_{n}\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
a_{n}^{*}a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}^{*}a_{n}\\
\end{bmatrix}\right)\\
&=\lVert a\rVert^{2}\langle\!\langle\xi,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}.\end{split}

(5.16)

In this calculation,
\vec{v}:=(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})\in{{\mathcal{K}}}\oplus\cdots\oplus{{\mathcal{K}}}
and
the norm
\lVert a\rVert
of
a
is the one from the
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
.
The third line in (
5.16
) follows from Lemma
5.1
and the inequality
|\omega(y^{*}xy)|\leq\lVert x\rVert\omega(y^{*}y)
for all
x,y
in a
C^{*}
-algebra and
\omega
a positive
linear functional on that
C^{*}
-algebra (see Proposition 2.1.5. part (ii) in
Dixmier
Dixmier 1977
for a proof of this inequality). In this case, this inequality is
applied to the positive linear functional

\omega:=s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}:{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathbb{C}}}
with
x
positive
so that
|\omega(y^{*}xy)|=\omega(y^{*}xy).

The fourth line in (
5.16
) holds because
the norm of
\mathrm{diag}(a^{*}a,\dots,a^{*}a)
in
{{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is
equal to
\lVert a^{*}a\rVert.

This is because every injective
∗
-homomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras is an isometry (cf. Propositions 1.3.7 and 1.8.1 in Dixmier
Dixmier 1977
). In this case, the
∗
-homomorphism is given by the function

\mathcal{A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{M}_{n}({{\mathcal{A}}})
sending
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
to
\mathrm{diag}(a,\dots,a).

The last line of (
5.16
) follows from the
C^{*}
-identity for
C^{*}
-algebras
and the definitions of
\langle\!\langle\xi,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}
and
s_{\varphi,\vec{v}}.

Thus, (
5.16
)
shows that
\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)
is bounded/continuous. If we write
\mathcal{B}(V)
for the algebra of bounded operators on a seminormed vector space
V
, then
\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)\in{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Furthermore, (
5.16
)
shows that
{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}
is an invariant subspace under the

\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}
action, meaning
\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)\zeta\in\mathcal{N}_{\varphi}
for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{N}_{\varphi}
and
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.
Therefore, the quotient space

({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}
has a well-defined action

\pi_{\varphi}:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{B}}}\big(({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}\big)
by Lemma
5.3
.
If an element of
({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}
is denoted by
[\xi]_{\varphi}
, or just
[\xi]
when working with a fixed OCP map
\varphi
,
this induced action of
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}
on
[\xi]
is given by

\pi_{\varphi}(a)[\xi]:=[\pi^{\prime}_{\varphi}(a)\xi].

(5.17)

By (
5.14
),
the sesquilinear form
\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}

descends to a well-defined inner product

\begin{split}({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}\times({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}&\xrightarrow{\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle_{\varphi}}{{\mathbb{C}}}\\
([\xi],[\zeta])&\xmapsto{\;\hskip 16.38895pt\;\;}\langle\!\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}.\end{split}

(5.18)

The fact that
\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle_{\varphi}

is positive definite follows from
(
5.11
) and the definition of

{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}
in (
5.13
).
Let

{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}:=\overline{({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}}

(5.19)

denote the completion of this topological vector space
with respect to the inner product
\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle_{\varphi}.

Since
\pi_{\varphi}(a)
is a bounded linear operator
on
({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi},

it extends uniquely to a bounded linear operator, also denoted by
\pi_{\varphi}(a),
on
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}.

Furthermore, since
\pi_{\varphi}(a)\in{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}),
it has an adjoint
\pi_{\varphi}(a)^{*}.

This adjoint satisfies

\begin{split}&\big\langle\pi_{\varphi}(a)^{*}[\xi],[\zeta]\big\rangle_{\varphi}=\big\langle[\xi],\pi_{\varphi}(a)[\zeta]\big\rangle_{\varphi}=\sum_{i,j}\big\langle v_{i},\varphi(a_{i}^{*}ab_{j})w_{j}\big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\\
&\quad=\sum_{i,j}\Big\langle v_{i},\varphi\big((a^{*}a_{i})^{*}b_{j}\big)w_{j}\Big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}=\sum_{i,j}\langle\!\langle a^{*}a_{i}\otimes v_{i},b_{j}\otimes w_{j}\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}=\big\langle\pi_{\varphi}(a^{*})[\xi],[\zeta]\big\rangle_{\varphi}\end{split}

(5.20)

for all
\xi=\big[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\big],\zeta=\big[\sum_{j}b_{j}\otimes w_{j}\big]\in({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/\mathcal{N}_{\varphi}.

By uniqueness of bounded adjoints,
\pi_{\varphi}(a^{*})=\pi_{\varphi}(a)^{*}.

Finally, note that
\pi_{\varphi}(1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}})=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}}.

Thus,
\pi_{\varphi}:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi})
defines a
∗
-homomorphism.

Now, set

\begin{split}{{\mathcal{K}}}&\xrightarrow{V_{\varphi}}{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}\\
w&\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\otimes w].\end{split}

(5.21)

Then,

\big\lVert V_{\varphi}(w)\big\rVert_{\varphi}^{2}=\big\lVert[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\otimes w]\big\rVert^{2}_{\varphi}=\big\langle w,\varphi(1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}^{*}1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}})w\big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}=\big\langle w,\varphi(1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}})w\big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\leq\big\lVert\varphi(1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}})\big\rVert\lVert w\rVert^{2}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}

(5.22)

for all
w\in{{\mathcal{K}}}.

The last inequality in (
5.22
) follows from Cauchy–Schwarz for
\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
and positivity of
\varphi
.
This proves that
V_{\varphi}
is bounded.
Note that if
\varphi
is unital, the inequality in (
5.22
) becomes an equality, which proves
V_{\varphi}
is an isometry.

This concludes Stinespring’s construction
of an anchored representation
from an OCP map,
i.e. the functor

\begin{split}\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}\\
(K,\varphi)&\xmapsto{\;\;\hskip 16.38895pt\;}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})\end{split}

(5.23)

on objects of
\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}}).
It restricts to a well-defined map
\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}\mathbf{PAnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}
.

ii.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)
be a morphism of OCP maps.
By Definition
2.4
,
this means
T:{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{L}}}
is a bounded linear map and

\psi(a)T=T\varphi(a)
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})
and
({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\psi},\pi_{\psi},V_{\psi})

be the corresponding Stinespring anchored representations
from the first step.
Set

{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}\xrightarrow{L^{\prime}_{T}:=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\otimes T}{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}}.

(5.24)

Then, for
\xi:=\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}},

\begin{split}\langle\!\langle L^{\prime}_{T}(\xi),L^{\prime}_{T}(\xi)\rangle\!\rangle_{\psi}&=\sum_{i,j}\langle T(v_{i}),\psi(a_{i}^{*}a_{j})T(v_{j})\rangle_{{{\mathcal{L}}}}\quad\text{by (\ref{eq:stineT}) and (\ref{eq:sesquilinear})}\\
&=\sum_{i,j}\langle T(v_{i}),T\varphi(a_{i}^{*}a_{j})v_{j}\rangle_{{{\mathcal{L}}}}\quad\text{by (\ref{eq:opstatesdiagram})}\\
&=\sum_{i,j}\langle v_{i},T^{*}T\varphi(a_{i}^{*}a_{j})v_{j}\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\\
&\leq\lVert T^{*}T\rVert\langle\!\langle\xi,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}\quad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem:bdd}}.\end{split}

(5.25)

Therefore,
L^{\prime}_{T}(\mathcal{N}_{\varphi})\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{\psi}
. Hence, Lemma
5.3
implies
L^{\prime}_{T}
descends to a
bounded
linear map
L_{T}:{({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{L}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\psi}}
,
which itself extends uniquely to a bounded linear map
L_{T}:{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\psi}.

Note that when
T
is an isometry,
L_{T}
is also an isometry.

(5.26)

follow directly from the definitions. However,

(5.27)

the last of the conditions in Definition
2.4
,
requires an argument. First, to find the formula for

V^{*}_{\varphi}:{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{K}}},
let

\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]_{\varphi}\in({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}

and
w\in{{\mathcal{K}}}.
Then

\begin{split}&\left\langle V^{*}_{\varphi}\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]_{\varphi},w\right\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}=\left\langle\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]_{\varphi},V_{\varphi}w\right\rangle_{\varphi}\\
&\qquad=\left\langle\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]_{\varphi},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\otimes w]_{\varphi}\right\rangle_{\varphi}=\sum_{i}\langle v_{i},\varphi(a_{i}^{*})w\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}=\left\langle\sum_{i}\varphi(a_{i})v_{i},w\right\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\end{split}

(5.28)

entails the formula

V^{*}_{\varphi}\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]_{\varphi}=\sum_{i}\varphi(a_{i})v_{i}.

(5.29)

Commutativity of the diagram (
5.27
) then
follows from

\begin{split}\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{1}&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{1}\\

on morphisms of OCP maps on
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\psi}}

\scriptstyle{L_{T}}
\scriptstyle{L_{S}}
\scriptstyle{L_{ST}}

(5.32)

commutes.

As a simple example, let
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}})
be an operator state with
\mathcal{N}_{\varphi}=\{0\}
and set
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}={{\mathbb{C}}}.
Then there is only a single (unital)

∗
-homomorphism
f:{{\mathbb{C}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
and
\varphi
gets pulled back to the map
\varphi\circ f
that sends
1\in{{\mathbb{C}}}
to
\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}.
In this case,
\mathcal{N}_{\varphi\circ f}=\{0\}
, which entails
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f}\cong{{\mathbb{C}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}\cong{{\mathcal{K}}}
, while
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}\cong{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}.

Nevertheless, there is a natural transformation

\textstyle{\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{OCP}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}_{f}}

(5.34)

defined as follows.
Given an OCP map
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
on
{{\mathcal{A}}},
applying

\mathbf{OCP}_{f}
followed by
\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}

provides the Stinespring anchored representation

({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f},\pi_{\varphi\circ f},V_{\varphi\circ f})

on
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}
,
while applying
\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
followed by
\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}

gives
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi}\circ f,V_{\varphi}).

The morphism
\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)

from the first to the latter
is given
by
(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},L_{f}),
where
L_{f}:{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}
is defined as the unique map associated to

{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}\xrightarrow{L^{\prime}_{f}:=f\otimes\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}}{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}

(5.35)

via Lemma
5.3
. This lemma applies because if

\xi=\sum_{i}a^{\prime}_{i}\otimes v_{i}\in{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}},

then

\begin{split}\langle\!\langle L^{\prime}_{f}(\xi),L^{\prime}_{f}(\xi)\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}&=\sum_{i,j}\Big\langle v_{i},\varphi\big(f(a^{\prime}_{i})^{*}f(a^{\prime}_{j})\big)v_{j}\Big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\quad\text{by definition of $\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi}$}\\
&=\sum_{i,j}\Big\langle v_{i},\varphi\big(f({a^{\prime}_{i}}^{*}a^{\prime}_{j})\big)v_{j}\Big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\quad\text{since $f$ is a ${}^{*}$-homomorphism}\\
&=\langle\!\langle\xi,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi\circ f}\quad\text{by definition of $\langle\!\langle\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;\rangle\!\rangle_{\varphi\circ f}$}.\end{split}

(5.36)

In fact, this calculation shows
L_{f}:{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}
is an isometry.
The requirements of a morphism of anchored representations all hold as well.
Furthermore,
\mathbf{Stine}_{f}
is a natural transformation
because for any morphism
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)
of
operator states on
{{\mathcal{A}}},
the diagram

\textstyle{({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f},\pi_{\varphi\circ f},V_{\varphi\circ f})}
\textstyle{({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi}\circ f,V_{\varphi})}
\textstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\psi\circ f},\pi_{\psi\circ f},V_{\psi\circ f})}
\textstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\psi},\pi_{\psi}\circ f,V_{\psi})}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},L_{f})}
\scriptstyle{(T,L_{T})}
\scriptstyle{(T,L_{T})}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{L}}}},L_{f})}

(5.37)

commutes. Finally,
\mathbf{Stine}_{f}:\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\circ\mathbf{OCP}_{f}\Rightarrow\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}\circ\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
restricts to a natural transformation

\mathbf{Stine}_{f}:\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\circ\mathbf{OpSt}_{f}\Rightarrow\mathbf{PAnRep}_{f}\circ\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}

since
\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)=(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},L_{f})
consists of two isometries by (
5.36
).

iv.

Oplax-naturality of
\mathbf{Stine}
holds because

\mathbf{Stine}_{\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
is the identity natural transformation
for every
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}

and
the two natural transformations
(after composition in the diagram on the right)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 70.59717pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-68.04161pt\raise 30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-70.59717pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.50891pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{OCP}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime\prime})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 14.1216pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 14.1216pt\raise-60.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 45.0pt\raise-51.99109pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.55489pt\raise-0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.74757pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}_{f^{\prime}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.55489pt\raise 0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.55489pt\raise-0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.98663pt\raise-8.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.55489pt\raise 0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.55489pt\raise-0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.55489pt\raise 0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.55489pt\raise-0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}

(5.38)

are equal
for every pair of composable
∗
-homomorphisms

{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}
.

v.

Fix a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and let

({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
be an anchored representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Applying the functor
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
followed by
\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}

to this representation gives

({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\xmapsto{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi)\xmapsto{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi},\pi_{\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi},V_{\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi}).

(5.39)

Set
m^{\prime}_{\pi,V}:{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}
to be the composite

\begin{split}{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}&\xrightarrow{\pi\otimes V}{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}})\otimes{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}\\
\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}&\xmapsto{\;\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447ptm^{\prime}_{\pi,V}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 16.38895pt\;}\sum_{i}\pi(a_{i})V(v_{i}),\end{split}

(5.40)

where the second map is the canonical action of
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}})
on
{{\mathcal{H}}}.

If

\xi=\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}},

then

\big\langle m^{\prime}_{\pi,V}(\xi),m^{\prime}_{\pi,V}(\xi)\big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}=\sum_{i,j}\big\langle v_{i},V^{*}\pi(a_{i}^{*}a_{j})Vv_{j}\big\rangle_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\\
=\langle\!\langle\xi,\xi\rangle\!\rangle_{\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi}.

(5.41)

Thus,
m^{\prime}_{\pi,V}
defines an isometry
m_{\pi,V}:{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}
by Lemma
5.3
.
Note that
m_{\pi,V}
is an isometry even though
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
need not be a
preserving
anchored representation.
Commutativity of the diagrams

0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!\!,\end{split}

(5.48)

i.e. for every object
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)

of
\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}})
with

\varphi:=\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi,

the diagram

\textstyle{\big({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f},\pi_{\varphi\circ f},V_{\varphi\circ f}\big)}
\textstyle{\big({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi}\circ f,V_{\varphi}\big)}
\textstyle{({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi\circ f,V)}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},L_{f})=\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\hskip 16.38895pt}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\hskip 16.38895pt\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}\big(m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\big)=(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},m_{\pi,V})}
\scriptstyle{m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi\circ f,V)=(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},m_{\pi\circ f,V})}

(5.49)

of morphisms of anchored representations of

{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}
must commute. This follows directly from the definitions.
Hence,
m
is a modification, which also restricts to a modification
when working with
\mathbf{OpSt}
and
\mathbf{PAnRep}
by (
5.41
).

vii.

To see that

\textstyle{\mathbf{OCP}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{OCP}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{OCP}}}

(5.50)

commutes, first fix a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Commutativity in (
5.50
) requires that

\textstyle{\mathbf{OCP}({{{\mathcal{A}}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{AnRep}({{{\mathcal{A}}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{OCP}({{{\mathcal{A}}}})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}({{{\mathcal{A}}}})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{OCP}({{{\mathcal{A}}}})}}

(5.51)

must commute.
On objects, this translates to

\varphi=\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}_{\varphi}}\circ\pi_{\varphi}
for
every OCP map
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
,
which follows from the definitions of
V_{\varphi}
and
\pi_{\varphi}
.
Since a morphism
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}({{\mathcal{L}}},\psi)
is unchanged
under
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\circ\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},
(
5.51
) commutes.

Commutativity of (
5.50
) also requires that for
every
∗
-homomorphism

(5.52)

i.e. to every OCP map
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi),
the diagram

\textstyle{\big({{\mathcal{K}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}_{\varphi\circ f}}\circ\pi_{\varphi\circ f}\big)}
\textstyle{\big({{\mathcal{K}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}_{\varphi}}\circ\pi_{\varphi}\circ f\big)}
\textstyle{({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi\circ f)}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}=\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi))\;\hskip 16.38895pt}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\hskip 8.19447pt\mathbf{OCP}_{f}\left(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}\right)=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}}

(5.53)

of morphisms of OCP maps on
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}
must commute,
which it clearly does. Commutativity of (
5.50
) with

\mathbf{OpSt}
and
\mathbf{PAnRep}
follows from this as well.

viii.

By the final remark in the appendix of
Parzygnat 2018
, it suffices to prove

(5.54)

and

\hskip-11.38109pt\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 93.59709pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.04161pt\raise 67.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern-12.47743pt\raise-22.00195pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 12.47743pt\raise-22.00195pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.80022pt\raise 6.7994pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.06172pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}

(5.55)

for each object
{{\mathcal{A}}}
of
\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}.

The equality in (
5.54
) follows from the equality

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big(\underbrace{m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)}_{(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},m_{\pi,V})}\big)=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}

(5.56)

of morphisms of OCP maps from
({{\mathcal{K}}},\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi)
to itself
for every anchored representation
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

To see the equality in (
5.55
),
consider an OCP map
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi).
Applying

\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\circ\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\circ\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}

to this gives
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})

because
\varphi=\mathrm{Ad}_{V_{\varphi}^{*}}\circ\pi_{\varphi}.

In order for (
5.55
) to hold, it should be the case that

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})=(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}})
as morphisms
from
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})
to
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})

in
\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}).
These are in fact equal because
for any element

\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]\in{({{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}})/{{\mathcal{N}}}_{\varphi}},

m_{\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi}}\left(\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]\right)=\sum_{i}\pi_{\varphi}(a_{i})V_{\varphi}(v_{i})=\sum_{i}\pi_{\varphi}(a_{i})[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\otimes v_{i}]=\sum_{i}[a_{i}\otimes v_{i}].

(5.57)

This proves that the quadruple
(\mathbf{Stine},\mathbf{rest},\mathrm{id},m)
is
an adjunction in
\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{CAT})
,
i.e.
\mathbf{Stine}
is left adjoint to
\mathbf{rest}.

6
Stinespring dilations and their universal properties

The left adjoint
\mathbf{Stine}
to
\mathbf{rest}
provides what is sometimes called
a
minimal Stinespring representation/dilation
of an operator-valued completely positive map
(for comparison, see the discussion after Theorem 4.1 in Paulsen
Paulsen 2003
).

Definition 6.1
.

A
Stinespring representation/dilation
of an OCP map

({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
is an anchored representation

({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
such that
\varphi=\mathrm{Ad}_{V^{*}}\circ\pi.

A Stinespring representation
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
is said to be
minimal

iff

\pi({{\mathcal{A}}})V({{\mathcal{K}}}):=\mathrm{span}\big\{\pi(a)V(v)\;:\;a\in{{\mathcal{A}}},\;v\in{{\mathcal{K}}}\big\}

(6.1)

is dense in
{{\mathcal{H}}}.

Remark 6.2
.

In terms of the functors and natural transformations introduced,

({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
is a Stinespring representation of
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
on
{{\mathcal{A}}}

if and only if
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)=({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi).

Minimality will be addressed in Corollary
6.8
.

The following corollaries explain the meaning of Theorem
5.5
more concretely.

Corollary 6.3
.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\xrightarrow{T}(\mathcal{L},\psi)
be a morphism of
OCP maps on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and let
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{I},\rho,W)
be any Stinespring representation of
\psi
.
Then there exists a unique morphism
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{I},\rho,W)
such that
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\mathcal{T})=T.

Furthermore, if
T
is a morphism of operator states, then
\mathcal{T}
is a morphism of preserving anchored representations.

Proof 6.4
.

Since
(\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}})},m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}})
is an adjunction, existence and uniqueness follows from the universal property
of adjunctions in part
i
of Lemma
B.8
.
In the notation of that lemma,

c=({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi),

d=(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{I},\rho,W),
and
g=T.

This unique morphism is given by

\mathcal{T}=\big(m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{I},\rho,W)\big)\circ\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(T)=(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{L}}}},m_{\rho,W})\circ(T,L_{T})=(T,m_{\rho,W}L_{T}).

(6.2)

Recall,
m_{\rho,W}L_{T}:{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{I}
is uniquely determined by
the assignment

{{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{K}}}/\mathcal{N}_{\varphi}\ni\left[\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right]_{\varphi}\mapsto\sum_{i}\rho(a_{i})W\big(T(v_{i})\big).

(6.3)

When
T
is a morphism of operator states, both
T
and
m_{\rho,W}L_{T}
are isometries. Hence,
\mathcal{T}
defines a morphism of preserving anchored representations.

The following is a special case of Corollary
6.3
. It will be used to provide the relationship to minimal Stinespring representations in Corollary
6.8
.

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
be an OCP map on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}

and let
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
be any Stinespring representation of
\varphi.

Then there exists a unique morphism
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
such that
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\mathcal{T})=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}.
In fact,
\mathcal{T}=(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},m_{\pi,V})
,
and therefore consists of two isometries.

Proof 6.6
.

This follows from Corollary
6.3
for
T=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
. The fact that
\mathcal{T}
consists of two isometries follows from the fact that
m_{\pi,V}
is an isometry for any anchored representation
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
by (
5.41
).

Remark 6.7
.

If
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
is an OCP map on
{{\mathcal{A}}},
one can form a category of Stinespring representations of
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi).
The objects of this category are Stinespring representations of
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
and morphisms are of the form
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},L)}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{I},\rho,W)
.
Corollary
6.5
then says that
\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
is an initial object in the category of Stinespring representations of
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi).

We now state the relationship between our adjunction and minimal Stinespring representations.

Corollary 6.8
.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
be an OCP map on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Then
\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\equiv({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})
is a minimal Stinespring representation of
\varphi.

Conversely, given any minimal Stinespring representation
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
of
\varphi,
there exists a unique
isomorphism
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
of anchored representations. In fact, this isomorphism consists of two unitaries.

Proof 6.9
.

you found me!

(
\Rightarrow
)
By Remark
6.2
,
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})
is a Stinespring representation due to part
vii
in the proof of Theorem
5.5
. By the construction in part
i
,

\mathrm{span}\big\{\pi_{\varphi}(a)V_{\varphi}(v)\;:\;a\in{{\mathcal{A}}},\;v\in{{\mathcal{K}}}\big\}=\mathrm{span}\big\{[a\otimes v]_{\varphi}\;:\;a\in{{\mathcal{A}}},\;v\in{{\mathcal{K}}}\big\},

(6.4)

which is dense in
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}
by definition (cf. (
5.19
)).
Hence,
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})
is minimal.

(
\Leftarrow
)
Conversely, let
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
be a minimal Stinespring representation
of
\varphi.
Then Corollary
6.5
provides the unique morphism

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)\equiv(\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}},m_{\pi,V})
from
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},V_{\varphi})
to
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}},\pi,V)
,
with
m_{\pi,V}
an isometry. Furthermore,
m_{\pi,V}
is unitary because the image of
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi}
under
m_{\pi,V}
is exactly
\pi({{\mathcal{A}}})V({{\mathcal{K}}})
by (
5.40
) and equals
{{\mathcal{H}}}
by the minimality assumption.

Theorem
5.5
entails the following with regards to morphisms and functoriality.

Corollary 6.10
.

Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
be an OCP map on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and let
f:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a morphism of
C^{*}
-algebras. Let
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f},\pi_{\varphi\circ f},V_{\varphi\circ f})=\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi\circ f)
be the minimal Stinespring representation of
\varphi\circ f
and let

be the pull-back of the minimal Stinespring representation
\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
of
\varphi
under
f.

Then

\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi):({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f},\pi_{\varphi\circ f},V_{\varphi\circ f})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi}\circ f,V_{\varphi})
is the unique morphism in
\mathbf{AnRep}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})
such that
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\right)=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}.
In fact,

\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)=m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\Big(\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}\big(\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\big)\Big).

(6.5)

Furthermore,
the assignment
f\mapsto\mathbf{Stine}_{f}
is functorial in the sense that

\mathbf{Stine}_{f\circ f^{\prime}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)=\big(\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\big)\circ\big(\mathbf{Stine}_{f^{\prime}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi\circ f)\big)

(6.6)

for all composable pairs
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}
of morphisms of
C^{*}
-algebras and for all OCP maps
\varphi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.61993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\!{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{K}}}).

Proof 6.11
.

Since
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi}\circ f,V_{\varphi})
is a Stinespring representation of
({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi\circ f),
Corollary
6.5
says there is a unique morphism from
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f},\pi_{\varphi\circ f},V_{\varphi\circ f})
to
({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi}\circ f,V_{\varphi})
that restricts to
\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{K}}}}
under
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}.
By construction (cf. Equation (
5.34
) and what follows),
\mathbf{Stine}_{f}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)
is one such morphism. Similarly (cf. Equations (
5.40
) and (
5.44
)),

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\Big(\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}\big(\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\big)\Big)
is another such morphism because the target of this morphism is

\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}\big(\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\big)=({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi}\circ f,V_{\varphi})

(6.7)

and the source of this morphism is

\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\Big(\underbrace{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\Big(\mathbf{AnRep}_{f}\big(\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi)\big)\Big)}_{({{\mathcal{K}}},\varphi\circ f)}\Big)=({{\mathcal{K}}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\varphi\circ f},\pi_{\varphi\circ f},V_{\varphi\circ f}).

(6.8)

Equation (
6.6
) is precisely oplax-naturality of
\mathbf{Stine}
from equality of the diagrams in (
5.38
).

Remark 6.12
.

Corollary
6.10
also shows that the definition and oplaxness of
\mathbf{Stine}
on morphisms of
C^{*}
-algebras is determined by (
6.5
),
which itself follows from the universal property of the adjunctions

(\mathbf{Stine}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{OpSt}({{\mathcal{A}}})},m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}})

over all
C^{*}
-algebras
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

This is discussed in more abstract form in Proposition
B.10
and Remark
B.13
.
